{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_4/reproducibility.md", "content": "# Reproducing this artifact (Ubuntu, Python 3.12, CPU only, 0 OpenAlex credits)\n\n1. Install uv, then create the environment:\n   `uv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python=.venv/bin/python requests numpy pandas scipy scikit-learn statsmodels networkx loguru matplotlib pyarrow`\n2. Download the free OpenAlex S3 sources snapshot (~370 MB on disk, no credits): `bash snapshot/download_sources.sh`\n3. Run the unit tests: `.venv/bin/python tests/test_units.py` (rarefaction vs Monte Carlo, Kleinberg spike).\n4. Run the analysis offline from the frozen API cache `cache/raw/`: `.venv/bin/python method.py` (about 6 min on 4 CPUs). Here we fetch the footer, work out the byte ranges of the needed column chunks, fetch\nthem concurrently, and serve them to pyarrow from memory through a file-like object (the workspace filesystem\ndoes not support sparse files, so a local sparse copy is not an option).\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport bisect\nimport io\nimport struct\nimport time\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\n\nimport pyarrow as pa\nimport pyarrow.parquet as pq\nimport requests\n\nS3_HTTP = \"https://openalex.s3.amazonaws.com/\"\n_session = requests.Session()\n_adapter = requests.adapters.HTTPAdapter(pool_connections=32, pool_maxsize=32)\n_session.mount(\"https://\", _adapter)\n\n\ndef _get_range(url: str, start: int, end: int) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"Inclusive byte range with retries.\"\"\"\n    err = None\n    for k in range(6):\n        if k:\n            time.sleep(2 * k)\n        try:\n            r = _session.get(url, headers={\"Range\": f\"bytes={start}-{end}\"}, timeout=120)\n            if r.status_code in (200, 206) and len(r.content) == end - start + 1:\n                return r.content\n            err = f\"HTTP {r.status_code} len={len(r.content)}\"\n        except requests.RequestException as e:\n            err = repr(e)\n    raise RuntimeError(f\"range fetch failed {url} {start}-{end}: {err}\")\n\n\nclass RangeFile(io.RawIOBase):\n    \"\"\"Read-only file object that serves bytes only from pre-fetched ranges.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, size: int, chunks: dict[int, bytes]):\n        super().__init__()\n        self._size = size\n        # merge overlapping / touching buffers so every request falls inside one buffer\n        merged: list[tuple[int, bytes]] = []\n        for s in sorted(chunks):\n            b = chunks[s]\n            if merged and s <= merged[-1][0] + len(merged[-1][1]):\n                ps, pb = merged[-1]\n                end = s + len(b)\n                if end > ps + len(pb):\n                    pb = pb + b[ps + len(pb) - s:]\n                merged[-1] = (ps, pb)\n            else:\n                merged.append((s, b))\n        self._chunks = dict(merged)\n        self._starts = [s for s, _ in merged]\n        self._pos = 0\n\n    def readable(self) -> bool:\n        return True\n\n    def seekable(self) -> bool:\n        return True\n\n    def tell(self) -> int:\n        return self._pos\n\n    def seek(self, pos: int, whence: int = 0) -> int:\n        if whence == 0:\n            self._pos = pos\n        elif whence == 1:\n            self._pos += pos\n        else:\n            self._pos = self._size + pos\n        return self._pos\n\n    def size(self) -> int:\n        return self._size\n\n    def read(self, n: int = -1) -> bytes:\n        if n is None or n < 0:\n            n = self._size - self._pos\n        n = min(n, self._size - self._pos)\n        i = bisect.bisect_right(self._starts, self._pos) - 1\n        if i < 0:\n            raise OSError(f\"offset {self._pos} not fetched\")\n        s = self._starts[i]\n        buf = self._chunks[s]\n        off = self._pos - s\n        if off + n > len(buf):\n            raise OSError(f\"range {self._pos}+{n} not fully fetched (chunk {s}+{len(buf)})\")\n        self._pos += n\n        return buf[off:off + n]\n\n    def readinto(self, b) -> int:\n        data = self.read(len(b))\n        b[:len(data)] = data\n        return len(data)\n\n\ndef read_columns(key: str, size: int, columns: list[str], n_threads: int = 12,\n                 merge_gap: int = 1 << 20) -> pa.Table:\n    \"\"\"Read `columns` (parquet leaf paths, e.g. 'topics.list.element.id') of the snapshot file `key`.\"\"\"\n    url = S3_HTTP + key\n    tail_len = min(size, 2 << 20)\n    tail = _get_range(url, size - tail_len, size - 1)\n    assert tail[-4:] == b\"PAR1\", \"not a parquet file\"\n    flen = struct.unpack(\"<I\", tail[-8:-4])[0]\n    if flen + 8 > tail_len:\n        tail_len = flen + 8\n        tail = _get_range(url, size - tail_len, size - 1)\n    chunks = {size - tail_len: tail}\n    meta = pq.ParquetFile(pa.PythonFile(RangeFile(size, dict(chunks)), mode=\"r\")).metadata\n    want = set(columns)\n    ranges = []\n    for rg in range(meta.num_row_groups):\n        r = meta.row_group(rg)\n        for c in range(r.num_columns):\n            col = r.column(c)\n            if col.path_in_schema in want:\n                start = col.data_page_offset\n                if col.has_dictionary_page and col.dictionary_page_offset and col.dictionary_page_offset > 0:\n                    start = min(start, col.dictionary_page_offset)\n                ranges.append((start, start + col.total_compressed_size - 1))\n    ranges.sort()\n    merged: list[list[int]] = []\n    for a, b in ranges:\n        if merged and a - merged[-1][1] <= merge_gap:\n            merged[-1][1] = max(merged[-1][1], b)\n        else:\n            merged.append([a, b])\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(n_threads) as ex:\n        datas = list(ex.map(lambda ab: _get_range(url, ab[0], ab[1]), merged))\n    for (a, _), d in zip(merged, datas):\n        chunks[a] = d\n    # collapse overlap with the tail chunk (tail is last; data ranges end before the footer)\n    pf = pq.ParquetFile(pa.PythonFile(RangeFile(size, chunks), mode=\"r\"))\n    return pf.read(columns=columns, use_threads=False)\n", "numLines": 143, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 143}}

{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_strat/current_report.md", "content": "# Do temporal network signals predict how scientific concepts spread across disciplines?\n\nThis report is the chronological record of a study that tests whether temporal changes in the structure of scientific knowledge networks can anticipate the emergence and cross-disciplinary integration of new scientific concepts. The study compares three families of candidate network indicators against a baseline of simple popularity and reach measures, evaluated on a panel of 46 to 48 concepts across four home-field groups (Biochemistry/Genetics, Computer Science, Engineering, Medicine) with onset years 2003 to 2009.\n\nThe primary outcome is rarefied field breadth (O2r), the expected number of distinct disciplines among a fixed-size random draw of a concept's papers in years t0+6 to t0+8. This outcome separates size-adjusted breadth from sheer volume. Secondary outcomes include sustained uptake and transience. Evaluation uses leave-one-group-out (LOGO) ridge regression with 2,000 stratified concept-level bootstraps, so that an indicator's incremental value (delta-rho or delta-AUC) is always measured on concepts from a home field the model has never seen.\n\nThree candidate indicators are tested, each representing a different theory of how concepts spread:\n\n- **Candidate L** (naturalisation gap, A\\*_h): a background-adjusted disciplinary self-citation index on the concept's lineage network, drawn from the epidemiological negative-control design [ARTIFACT:art_xp8BGBJZsxeI].\n- **Candidate D** (structural diversity of co-occurrence ties): the number of distinct Leiden communities a concept's new neighbours reach on a corpus-wide topic co-occurrence backbone [ARTIFACT:art_yrradSC27HtQ].\n- **Candidate G** (gateway landing): the eigenvector centrality of the adopting fields on a topic co-assignment backbone, weighted by early off-home share [ARTIFACT:art_33_KKk_G8Gw5].\n\n---\n\n# Iteration 1\n\n## 1. Strategy\n\nThe hypothesis predicts that a new concept spreads durably across disciplines when the fields that adopt it begin citing the concept's literature the way they cite their own literature, rather than reaching back to the concept's home field. The predicted signal is the \"naturalisation gap\" A\\*_h: the log odds ratio of the concept's citing-layer-by-cited-layer mixing table (off-home versus home), minus the same log odds ratio computed on the same citing papers' non-concept references. A negative value means adopters still import the concept across field lines more than their general citing habits predict; a value near or above zero means the concept's lineage follows the adopters' own field boundaries.\n\nTwo alternative hypotheses compete. The first is that the structural diversity of co-occurrence ties matters: concepts that acquire neighbours in many different Leiden communities on a corpus-wide backbone will spread more broadly, following complex-contagion theory [1, 2]. The second is that what matters is where a concept lands: concepts adopted early by high-centrality \"gateway\" fields on a topic-relatedness backbone will spread, following the principle of relatedness from economic complexity [3].\n\nAll three candidates are tested against a shared five-feature baseline: log early volume, publication growth, off-home share, Shannon entropy and field reach, all computed over the first five years from onset. The shared evaluation protocol defines onset, outcomes and panel membership across all three experiments. The pre-registered decision rule requires delta-rho >= 0.10 with 90% bootstrap CI excluding zero, the same sign in at least three of four home-field groups, split-half reliability >= 0.60 and absolute Spearman with log volume and growth <= 0.60.\n\n## 2. Data infrastructure and deviations\n\nThe shared OpenAlex credit pool (10,000 daily credits, split across five artifacts) was exhausted partway through iteration 1. This forced a data deviation that affects all three experiments:\n\n- **Yearly counts** (for onset, sustained uptake, transience, volume and growth) come from OpenAlex group-by calls and follow the shared evaluation protocol exactly for all 78 panel concepts.\n- **Field labels, concept papers and citation lineage** come from Semantic Scholar, a free source. Semantic Scholar's field assignments use a 23-field text-classifier taxonomy (s2-fos), which is concept-independent (it reads titles and abstracts, not references). The 23-field Semantic Scholar taxonomy was mapped to the 26-field OpenAlex scheme for the experiments that require venue labels.\n- **Background references** come from free OpenAlex singleton GET calls (verified zero-credit via response headers).\n- **Agreement between sources** on the 11 concepts where both sources have full data: Spearman correlation of rarefied breadth between outcomes labelled by Semantic Scholar and outcomes labelled by OpenAlex is 0.87.\n\nThe panel comprises 78 concepts with onset years 2003 to 2014, of which 46 to 48 fall in the dev window (onset 2003 to 2009, home field in one of the four groups). The exact number varies by experiment because each has slightly different eligibility filters.\n\n## 3. Experiment 1: Does the naturalisation gap predict cross-field spread? [ARTIFACT:art_xp8BGBJZsxeI]\n\n### 3.1 Construction\n\nFor each concept, the analysis downloads up to 25,000 phrase-matched papers and their citation lists. A concept lineage link is a citation from a concept-paper to an earlier concept-paper within three years. Links between papers that share an author are removed from the main estimator (self-lineage share ranged from 5% to 39% across concepts). The naturalisation gap A\\*_h is the Mantel-Haenszel pooled log odds ratio (a stratum-weighted average across yearly mixing tables) of the off-home/home mixing table for concept citations, minus the same log odds ratio for the same citing papers' non-concept references (the background term). The background term absorbs the adopters' general disciplinary homophily without modelling it.\n\nField labels for the lineage analysis come from Semantic Scholar's fractional field-of-study classifier. Home field is defined as the field(s) holding at least 40% of a concept's first 30 grounded papers. Concepts whose Semantic Scholar home field falls outside the four dev groups (Computer Science, Engineering, Biology, Medicine) are dropped (\"sealed\"), leaving 48 dev concepts (Biochemistry/Genetics 13, Computer Science 21, Engineering 3, Medicine 11).\n\n### 3.2 Measurement result: background homophily dominates lineage\n\nThe first finding is the background-homophily measurement result, which was predicted by the 8-concept probe. Among all 48 dev concepts, 100% have a positive background log odds ratio: every field's citing papers preferentially cite their own field's literature above chance. The coefficient of determination (R-squared) of the raw concept lineage log odds ratio on the background log odds ratio is 0.66 (90% CI [0.39, 0.83]), with Spearman correlation 0.70.\n\nWe define \"lineage autonomy\" as the degree to which a concept's citation chains stay within adopters' own disciplines rather than reaching back to the home field. Two-thirds of the between-concept variance in this raw lineage autonomy is explained by which fields adopt the concept and how insular those fields are in general. Uniform-null lineage indicators (including the study's own earlier A\\* and naive R_away) are therefore largely measures of field composition, not concept-specific rooting. This is the background-homophily measurement result.\n\n| Statistic | Value | 90% CI |\n|---|---|---|\n| R-squared of raw lineage LOR on background LOR | 0.66 | [0.39, 0.83] |\n| Spearman of raw lineage LOR with background LOR | 0.70 | - |\n| Share of concepts with positive background LOR | 100% (48/48) | - |\n| Share where background >= raw lineage LOR | 77% (37/48) | - |\n\n[FIGURE:fig_m1_scatter]\n\n### 3.3 Predictive screen: A\\*_h does not survive\n\nThe naturalisation gap A\\*_h was tested as a predictor of rarefied breadth (O2r, m = 30) in LOGO ridge regression. The five-feature baseline alone reaches rho = 0.834 with rarefied breadth. Adding A\\*_h produces delta-rho = -0.006 (90% CI [-0.034, 0.017]). A\\*_h fails the pre-registered rule on all three testable clauses:\n\n| Clause | Required | Observed | Pass? |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Delta-rho >= 0.10 and CI low > 0 | >= 0.10 | -0.006, CI [-0.034, 0.017] | No |\n| Positive in >= 3 of 4 groups | >= 3 | 0 of 4 (Bio 0.00, CS -0.003, Eng insufficient, Med 0.00) | No |\n| Split-half reliability (Spearman-Brown) | >= 0.60 | 0.58 | No |\n| Abs Spearman with log volume and growth | <= 0.60 | 0.14 (volume), 0.18 (growth) | Yes |\n\nThe size-independence clause passes: A\\*_h is not a proxy for concept volume or growth. But the gap adds nothing to the baseline on held-out fields, and it is not measured reliably enough (split-half r_SB = 0.58, just below the bar).\n\n### 3.4 Within-field heterogeneity and reliability gradient\n\nA\\*_h's sign flips across fields. The median A\\*_h in Medicine is +0.45 (naturalised), while in Computer Science it is -0.18 (borrowed). This within-field heterogeneity means A\\*_h is partly a field-composition indicator itself, despite the background adjustment.\n\nReliability depends on sample size. Concepts with fewer than 60 off-home children have split-half reliability below 0.40, while the 11 concepts with 60 or more off-home children reach r_SB = 0.72. On those 11 concepts, the eligible-subset delta-rho is +0.118 (90% CI [0.00, 0.36]), but this is too underpowered to interpret.\n\n| Off-home children bin | N concepts | Split-half r | Spearman-Brown |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 0-15 | 21 | 0.24 | 0.34 |", "numLines": 80, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 324}}

Returns states and burst weight.\"\"\"\n    r = np.asarray(r, float)\n    d = np.asarray(d, float)\n    n = len(r)\n    p0 = r.sum() / d.sum()\n    p1 = min(s * p0, 0.9999)\n\n    def cost(p):\n        return -(r * math.log(p) + (d - r) * math.log(1 - p))\n    c = np.vstack([cost(p0), cost(p1)])\n    trans = gamma * math.log(n)\n    V = np.zeros((2, n))\n    back = np.zeros((2, n), int)\n    V[0, 0], V[1, 0] = c[0, 0], c[1, 0] + trans\n    for t in range(1, n):\n        for q in (0, 1):\n            cand = [V[0, t - 1] + (trans if q == 1 else 0), V[1, t - 1]]\n            back[q, t] = int(np.argmin(cand))\n            V[q, t] = min(cand) + c[q, t]\n    st = [int(np.argmin(V[:, -1]))]\n    for t in range(n - 1, 0, -1):\n        st.append(back[st[-1], t])\n    st = st[::-1]\n rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)\n    rs = []\n    for _ in range(n_splits):\n        a, b = [], []\n        for labels in mats:\n            idx = rng.permutation(len(labels))\n            h = for i, f in enumerate(self.fields)}\n        self.phi = np.array(b[\"phi\"])\n        self.phi_min = np.array(b[\"phi_min\"])\n        self.gate = {k: np.array(b[k]) for k in (\"gateway_eig\", \"gateway_deg\", \"gateway_btw\", \"gateway_eig_phimin\")}\n        self.domain = b[\"domain\"]\n        self.logsize = np.log(np.array(b[\"n_field\"]))\n\n    def g(self, f: str, kind: str = \"gateway_eig\") -> float:\n        return float(self.gate[kind][self.idx[f]])\n\n\ndef g_family(fc: dict, home: list[str], bb: Backbone) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"G and secondaries from a field-count dict (labelled papers).\"\"\"\n    tot = sum(fc.values())\n    out = {}\n    off = {f: n for f, n in fc.items() if f not in home and n > 0}\n    offt = sum(off.values())\n    for kind, nm in ((\"gateway_eig\", \"G\"), (\"gateway_deg\", \"G_deg\"), (\"gateway_btw\", \"G_btw\"),\n                     (\"gateway_eig_phimin\", \"G_phimin\")):\n        out[nm] = sum(n * bb.g(f, kind) for f, n in off.items()) / offt if offt else math.nan\n    out[\"G_all\"] = sum(n * bb.g(f) for f, n in fc.items()) / tot if tot else math.nan\n    hi = [bb.idx[h] for h in home if h in bb.idx]\n    out[\"REL_home\"] = (sum(n * np.mean([bb.phi[i, bb.idx[f]] for i in hi]) for f, n in off.items()) / offt\n                       if offt and hi else math.nan)\n    if tot:\n        p = np.zeros(26)\n        for f, n in fc.items():\n            p[bb.idx[f]] = n / tot\n        D = 1 - bb.phi_min\n        np.fill_diagonal(D, 0)\n        out[\"RS\"] = float(p @ D @ p)\n        for dom in (\"Physical\", (\"Physical\", \"Life\", \"Health\", \"Social\"):\n            out[f\"DOM_{dom}\"] = math.nan\n    top5 = set(np.argsort(bb.gate[\"gateway_eig\"])[::-1][:5])\n    out[\"GATEWAY_REACH\"] = sum(1 for f, n in fc.items() if n >= 2 and bb.idx[f] in top5)\n    return out\n\n\ndef g_from_labels(labels: list[str], home: list[str], bb: Backbone) -> float:\n    return g_family(Counter(labels), home, bb)[\"G\"]\n\n\ndef label_indicators(fc: dict, home: list[str], total: int) -> dict:\n    lab = sum(fc.values())\n    off = sum(n for f, n in fc.items() if f not in home)\n    return {\"entropy\": shannon(fc) if lab else math.nan,\n            \"reach\": sum(1 for n in fc.values() if n >= 2),\n            \"offhome_share\": off / lab if lab else math.nan,\n            \"log_offhome_volume\": math.log1p(off),\n            \"label_coverage\": lab / total if total else math.nan}\n\n\ndef count_indicators(yc: dict, gtot: dict, t0: int, end: int) -> dict:\n    ys = list(range(t0, end + 1))\n 1) / (yc.get(t0 + 1, 0) + 1))}\n    x = np.array(ys, float) - t0\n    out[\"accel\"] = float(np.polyfit(x, np.log1p(n), 2)[0]) if len(ys) >= 3 else math.nan\n    yrs = list(range(t0 - 3, end + 1))\n    _, w = kleinberg_batched([yc.get(y, 0) for y in yrs], [gtot[y] for y in yrs])\n    out[\"burst\"] = w\n    return out\n\n\ndef outcomes(yc: dict, gtot: dict, t0: int, fcD: dict | None) -> dict:\n {"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_4/s0_labels.py", "content": "\"\"\"S0(c)-(d): venue-field labels per concept window, home field, dev gate.\n\nWindows (pooled, one group_by=primary_location.source.id call each, top-200 sources, max_pages from config):\n  A = t0..t0+1 (home), B = t0+2 (A+B = W3, G window), C = t0+3..t0+4 (A+B+C = W5), D = t0+6..t0+8 (outcome).\nBudget deviation (logged): per-year pulls were pooled into these 4 windows because the shared key had only\n~2,100 credits left for five artifacts; the next-field entry test uses the step A -> B -> C -> D.\n\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom collections import Counter\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom loguru import logger\n\nimport oa_client as oa\nfrom panel import query\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\nLAB_FILE = ROOT / \"cache\" / \"window_labels.json\"\nDEV_FIELDS = [\"Computer Science\", \"Engineering\", \"Biochemistry, Genetics and Molecular Biology\", \"Medicine\"]\nGROUP_SHORT = {\"Computer Science\": \"CS\", \"Engineering\": \"Eng\",\n               \"Biochemistry, Genetics and Molecular Biology\": \"BGM\", \"Medicine\": \"Med\"}\n\n\ndef windows(t0: int) -> dict[str, tuple[int, int]]:\n    return {\"A\": (t0, t0 + 1), \"B\": (t0 + 2, t0 + 2), \"C\": (t0 + 3, t0 + 4), \"D\": (t0 + 6, t0 + 8)}\n\n\ndef pull_window(concept_entry: str, t0: int, w: str, tag: str, max_pages: int = 1) -> dict:\n    y0, y1 = windows(t0)[w]\n    yr = f\"{y0}\" if y0 == y1 else f\"{y0}-{y1}\"\n    return oa.group_by_all(query(concept_entry) + f\",publication_year:{yr}\", \"primary_location.source.id\",\n                           tag=tag, max_pages=max_pages)\n\n\ndef field_counts(res: dict) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Map a source group_by result to field counts using the SRC cache.\"\"\"\n    fc: Counter = Counter()\n    lab = 0\n    for sid, n in res[\"groups\"].items():\n        f = oa.src_field(sid)\n        if f:\n            fc[f] += n\n            lab += n\n    return {\"fields\": dict(fc), \"labelled\": lab, \"total\": res[\"meta_count\"], \"top200_covered\":\n            sum(res[\"groups\"].values()), \"truncated_share\": res[\"truncated_share\"], \"complete\": res[\"complete\"],\n            \"n_sources\": len(res[\"groups\"])}\n\n\ndef home_of(fc: Counter) -> list[str]:\n    tot = sum(fc.values())\n    if not tot:\n        return []\n    h = [f for f, n in fc.items() if n / tot >= 0.40]\n    return sorted(h) if h else [fc.most_common(1)[0][0]]\n\n\ndef pull_many(jobs: list[tuple[str, str, int, str]], tag: str, max_pages: int = 1) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"jobs: (concept_name, entry, t0, window) -> {(name, w): raw result}; stops cleanly on BudgetStop.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n\n    def one(j):\n        nm, entry, t0, w = j\n        try:\n            return j, pull_window(entry, t0, w, f\"{tag}:{nm}:{w}\", max_pages=max_pages)\n        except oa.BudgetStop as e:\n            logger.warning(f\"BudgetStop {nm} {w}: {e}\")\n            return j, None\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(3) as ex:\n        for j, r in ex.map(one, jobs):\n            if r is not None:\n                out[(j[0], j[3])] = r\n    return out\n", "numLines": 76, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 76}}

{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/snapshot_meta.py", "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Free metadata from the OpenAlex snapshot (0 credits).\n\nSOURCE_FIELD[sid] = OpenAlex field (26-level) holding >= 40% of the source's summed topic counts, else None;\nrepositories are unlabelled (S0 rule, same as the run's probe). TOPIC_META[tid] = (name, subfield, field).\nWrites results/source_field.parquet and results/topic_meta.csv.\"\"\"\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport sys\nfrom collections import defaultdict\n\nimport pandas as pd\nimport pyarrow.parquet as pq\nfrom loguru import logger\n\nfrom config import LOGS, RES, SNAP, SRC_FIELD_SHARE\n\nlogger.remove()\nlogger.add(sys.stdout, level=\"INFO\", format=\"{time:HH:mm:ss}|{level:<7}|{message}\")\nlogger.add(LOGS / \"snapshot_meta.log\", rotation=\"30 MB\", level=\"DEBUG\")\n\n\ndef _num(x: str) -> int:\n    return int(x.rstrip(\"/\").split(\"/\")[-1].lstrip(\"STFfsd\").lstrip(\"ields/\").lstrip(\"ubfields/\"))\n\n\ndef topic_meta() -> pd.DataFrame:\n    rows = {}\n    for f in sorted((SNAP / \"topics\").rglob(\"*.parquet\")):  # sorted by updated_date -> latest wins\n        t = pq.read_table(f, columns=[\"id\", \"display_name\", \"subfield\", \"field\", \"keywords\"]).to_pylist()\n        for r in t:\n            tid = int(r[\"id\"].split(\"/T\")[-1])\n            rows[tid] = {\"topic\": tid, \"name\": r[\"display_name\"],\n                         \"subfield\": int(r[\"subfield\"][\"id\"].split(\"/\")[-1]), \"subfield_name\": r[\"subfield\"][\"display_name\"],\n                         \"field\": int(r[\"field\"][\"id\"].split(\"/\")[-1]), \"field_name\": r[\"field\"][\"display_name\"],\n                         \"keywords\": \"; \".join(r.get(\"keywords\") or [])}\n    return pd.DataFrame(sorted(rows.values(), key=lambda r: r[\"topic\"]))\n\n\ndef source_field() -> pd.DataFrame:\n    latest: dict[int, tuple] = {}\n    files = sorted((SNAP / \"sources\").rglob(\"*.parquet\"))\n    for f in files:\n        t = pq.read_table(f, columns=[\"id\", \"type\", \"topics\"])\n        ids = t.column(\"id\").to_pylist()\n        types = t.column(\"type\").to_pylist()\n        tops = t.column(\"topics\").to_pylist()\n        for sid, ty, tp in zip(ids, types, tops):\n            s = int(sid.split(\"/S\")[-1])\n            c: dict[int, int] = defaultdict(int)\n            for x in tp or []:\n                c[int(x[\"field\"][\"id\"].split(\"/\")[-1])] += int(x.get(\"count\") or 0)\n            latest[s] = (ty, dict(c))\n        del t\n    out = []\n    for s, (ty, c) in latest.items():\n        tot = sum(c.values())\n        lab = None\n        share = None\n        if tot > 0:\n            fbest = max(c, key=c.get)\n            share = c[fbest] / tot\n            if share >= SRC_FIELD_SHARE and ty != \"repository\":\n                lab = fbest\n        out.append({\"source\": s, \"type\": ty, \"field\": lab, \"top_share\": share, \"topic_total\": tot})\n    df = pd.DataFrame(out)\n    df[\"field\"] = df[\"field\"].astype(\"Int64\")\n    return df\n\n\n@logger.catch(reraise=True)\ndef main() -> None:\n    tm = topic_meta()\n    tm.to_csv(RES / \"topic_meta.csv\", index=False)\n    logger.info(f\"topics: {len(tm)}  fields: {tm.field.nunique()}  subfields: {tm.subfield.nunique()}\")\n    sf = source_field()\n    sf.to_parquet(RES / \"source_field.parquet\", index=False)\n    logger.info(f\"sources: {len(sf)}  labelled: {sf.field.notna().sum()}  types: {sf.type.value_counts().head(6).to_dict()}\")\n    fields = tm.drop_duplicates(\"field\")[[\"field\", \"field_name\"]].sort_values(\"field\")\n    fields.to_csv(RES / \"field_names.csv\", index=False)", "numLines": 80, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 85}}

{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_Id7TLZ6r1C7M/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/README.md", "content": "# Co-occurrence screen: structural diversity (D) and frequency-free selectivity (F)\n\nAI Inventor, invention loop iteration 1, artifact `gen_art_experiment_3` (wide screen, strategy `gen_strat_1`).\n\n**Question.** Do early *co-occurrence* signals of a newly emerging concept predict whether it later becomes\nbroadly integrated across science (size-adjusted venue-field breadth, **O2r**), beyond simple count, growth and\nreach baselines (**B5**)? The two candidates make opposite predictions:\n\n* **D**: *structural diversity* of newly acquired topic neighbours, meaning how many distinct communities of the\n  topic knowledge network they reach, normalised by a frequency-matched null. D predicts that the gain\n  concentrates on breadth.\n* **F**: *frequency-free selectivity*, meaning growth of the mean PMI of the top-20 topic neighbours minus a\n  size-matched, concept-preserving null. F predicts equal gains for uptake and breadth.\n\nBoth are scored on the frozen 78-concept dev panel **P78** under the shared protocol **S0** and the pre-registered\nselection rule: Delta-rho >= 0.10, 90% CI low > 0, >= 3/4 groups positive, split-half SB >= 0.6, and |rho| <= 0.6\nwith log early volume and with early growth.\n\n## Headline results (47 dev concepts: BIO 16, CS 12, MED 10, ENG 9; all have O2r)\n\n| candidate | Delta-rho vs B5 (O2r, LOGO) | 90% CI | groups + | split-half SB | size rho (logvol / growth) | survives |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **D** = `D_ratio` (primary D, pre-declared T3 fallback) | +0.006 | [-0.092, +0.135] | 3/4 (BIO +0.19, CS +0.01, ENG -0.08, MED +0.01) | 0.83 | 0.11 / 0.02 | no |\n| **F** = `F_res` | -0.060 | [-0.158, +0.014] | 1/4 (CS -0.22) | 0.44 | 0.04 / -0.09 | no |\n| `D_z` (the plan's literal primary; superseded) | +0.017 | [-0.101, +0.087] | 4/4 | 0.90 | **-0.63** / -0.09 | no (size relabel) |\n\n* **The B5 baseline is strong.** Its LOGO Spearman with O2r is 0.77 (early venue entropy alone reaches 0.70),\n  its AUC is 0.80 for O1 (uptake) and 0.86 for the top tercile of O2r.\n* **No candidate survives.** Per the pre-registered rule, the top-ranked candidate by Delta-rho (**D**) is\n  carried forward as the best available result, and the null is reported.\n* **Uptake/breadth dissociation.** Both verdicts are *inconclusive*. For D, dAUC(O2r_top) - dAUC(O1) = -0.017,\n  90% CI [-0.083, 0.075]. For F it is -0.081, 90% CI [-0.181, 0.045], so F's equivalence prediction is not met.\n* **O3 (transience) is not estimable** under leave-one-group-out: all 4 transient concepts (pandemic H1N1,\n  SARS, NOTES, single-incision laparoscopic surgery) have Medicine as home. The reach30 hurdle is not estimable\n  either, because every dev concept reaches N >= 30.\n* **Field-level retention R_j** (129 concept x off-home field rows, base AUC 0.76). Adding Dj gives dAUC +0.000,\n  90% CI [-0.044, 0.028]; adding Fj gives -0.008, 90% CI [-0.088, 0.034].\n* **Indicator portability.** Several co-occurrence breadth indicators are associated with O2r in *every* group,\n  but they are not incremental under the screen statistic:\n  * `D_ratio`: pooled rho 0.53 (0.33-0.63 per group);\n  * `D_rare`: pooled rho 0.63;\n  * `participation`: pooled rho 0.51;\n  * `NOV_res`: pooled rho 0.45.\n\n  **Negative result:** degree growth, strength growth and new-edge rate work in CS/AI only (CS rho about +0.45,\n  BIO and ENG negative), so they are flagged `CS-only`. Kendall's W of indicator ranks across the 4 groups is 0.52.\n* **Exploratory, not pre-registered.** With B5 at rho 0.77, Delta-rho is close to its ceiling: in-sample, adding\n  D_ratio raises it by only 0.016 even though D_ratio's partial Spearman with O2r given B5 is 0.47. An\n  out-of-group *partial* association (residuals on B5 fitted on the training groups) gives:\n  * D_ratio +0.34, 90% CI [0.02, 0.65], 3/4 groups positive;\n  * F_res -0.27.\n\n  A permutation test puts the D_ratio value at p = 0.037 (one-sided, 1,000 permutations), which is marginal.\n  This suggests D-type diversity carries non-redundant but modest information. The next iteration should use a\n  statistic that is not saturated by a strong baseline. This analysis is never used for selection.\n\nEvery number above comes from `results/screen_result.json`, `results/exploratory_partial_association.json` and\n`method_out.json`.\n\n## What was done (and how it departs from the plan)\n\nThe shared OpenAlex key had **0 credits left** when this artifact started (`x-ratelimit-remaining: 0`, resetting\nin about 11.7 h), so the design was adapted to be **almost credit-free**. Every change is logged in\n`results/deviations.json`.\n\n1. **S0 grounding via the API (156 credits, public anonymous pool).** For each concept, one\n   `group_by=publication_year` call with quoted `title_and_abstract.search`, `type:article|review` and\n   `is_paratext:false`. These counts give t0, the newborn flag, the dev cohort (2003 <= t0 <= 2009), O1, O3,\n   log volume and growth, exactly as in S0.\n2. **Everything else from the free OpenAlex S3 works snapshot (0 credits).** `scan_snapshot.py` streamed 7 leaf", "numLines": 70, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 195}}

"title": "How concepts hop between fields over time",
"summary": "RQ2 and the H1 mechanism, on a frame of about 400-1,500 newborn concepts built independently from the zero-credit OpenAlex S3 works snapshot with the S1 recipe. The frame uses the legacy-concept lexicon, title phrase matching, legacy-tag grounding plus a small labelled sense-filter benchmark, venue-field labels, and the frozen iteration-1 26-field backbone and gateway centrality. Four tests: (1) H2 next-field entry. A conditional logit on concept-year risk sets asks whether gateway-weighted relatedness to the fields that currently RETAIN a concept adds to relatedness-to-home, field size, Hidalgo density and the target field's own centrality. The LR test is frozen on dev and run once on held-out data, with label-permutation and rewired-backbone placebos. (2) Rescue. Are retained gateway episodes fed by re-import from non-home neighbour fields (citation provenance, background-adjusted, shared-author links removed; a Hanski-style connectivity to occupied neighbour fields), and does connectivity mediate the gateway effect on retention? (3) Relay. Do retained gateway episodes seed later field entries beyond what availability predicts? (4) Trajectories without predefined classes. DTW k-medoids and a Gaussian HMM on yearly field-state series, with k chosen by silhouette and bootstrap ARI. An ordering test asks whether the first retained gateway precedes entropy take-off, using a detector calibrated to a 5% false-alarm rate, a peripheral-field matched contrast and lead-lag panels. Spend: at most 300 API credits (cap 1,500) and at most $0.50 of OpenRouter. The pod is cpu_plus.",
"runpod_compute_profile": "cpu_plus",
"domain_practice": "WHAT I READ: iteration-1 code and outputs in the run tree: gen_art_experiment_4 (backbone.py, next_field.py, features.py, s0_ground.py, s0_labels.py, reproducibility.md), gen_art_experiment_3 (scan_snapshot.py, rangefile.py, snapshot_meta.py, config.py, README.md, .aii/manifest.yaml) and the iter_2 strategy report. Web: the relatedness-entry literature (Tartari/Chinazzi-style research-space diversification studies, Sci Rep 2020 'Knowledge and social relatedness shape research portfolio diversification'; Chinazzi et al. 2019 EPJ Data Science 'Mapping the physics research space') and the OpenAlex parquet snapshot notes (parquet added June 2026; abstract_inverted_index is a MAP column that loaders usually exclude). No domain handbook covers scientometrics, so this rests on those sources and on standard knowledge of the literatures named below.\n\n(1) BASELINES A REVIEWER EXPECTS. Next-entry (diversification) studies in economic complexity and science-of-science (Hidalgo et al. 2007; Neffke, Henning & Boschma 2011; Guevara et al. 2016; Chinazzi et al. 2019; the 2020 Sci Rep portfolio study) always model entry of a not-yet-active unit into a category at t as a function of RELATEDNESS DENSITY to the categories it already holds at t-1. They control for category size or ubiquity and sometimes the unit's own size. The model is a linear probability model with unit-by-time fixed effects or a conditional logit, with SEs clustered by unit. The first baseline a reviewer names is relatedness density itself (Hidalgo). The next are the target category's size and ubiquity, since big fields get entered more; iteration 1 found log size (AUC 0.74) beats density (0.61). To be fair, the rival must use the same backbone (the same phi) and the same risk sets as the candidate, and all regressors must be standardised. For a claim that POSITION rather than relatedness matters, reviewers also want the target field's own centrality as a control, a label-permutation null, and a configuration or rewired null for the centrality score.\n\n(2) DATA. OpenAlex is now standard: open, 476M works, venue sources with topic profiles, referenced_works and author ids. Its known issues, recorded in this run: field-dependent reference coverage; low venue-label coverage for conference-heavy CS (26-80%); legacy concept tags are MAG-classifier output (deprecated but still present); document-type noise. The OpenAlex topics/primary_topic classifier reads the paper's own text and references, so it is used only as a sensitivity label. Curated emergence lists (Research Fronts) are proprietary. MeSH is biomedical only.\n\n(3) CONTROLS. Entry models hold the backbone fixed in a pre-period to avoid simultaneity (here 1998-2002, frozen in iteration 1). Retention or extinction studies from metapopulation ecology (Hanski 1994 incidence function model; Brown & Kodric-Brown 1977 rescue effect) test rescue by showing that local extinction probability FALLS with connectivity to occupied patches, S_i = sum_j w_ij * occupied_j. They hold patch size (area) fixed, because big patches both persist and are well connected; the scientometric analogue is field size. The confound this design is most likely to be caught on: gateway fields are big, generically retentive and adopt everything (iteration-1 M1: 66-72% of raw lineage is homophily). So every mechanism statistic must be (a) within-concept (concept FE), (b) size-controlled, and (c) background-adjusted for citation provenance (same citing papers' non-concept references).\n\n(4) HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Entry studies use thousands to hundreds of thousands of unit-category-period rows and report clustered CIs. With 26 fields a concept-year risk set has at most 25 alternatives, so power comes from concepts: >= 300 concepts and >= 2,000 entry events are needed for stable conditional-logit coefficients with concept-clustered bootstrap CIs. Iteration 1 had 28-46 concepts and is not believed. Trajectory clustering is believed only with a stability check (bootstrap ARI >= 0.6) and a reported silhouette; k chosen visually is not accepted. Ordering claims need a detector calibrated to a false-alarm rate plus a threshold-free lead-lag panel, because threshold detectors produce spurious orderings when the series have different noise.\n\n(5) REPORTING. Conditional-logit or LPM coefficient tables with standardised coefficients and concept-clustered 95% CIs. LR tests of nested blocks. Mean within-stratum AUC of each block. Per-domain estimates pooled by random-effects meta-analysis (DerSimonian-Laird with I^2) plus a sign count. Placebo distributions (permutation p). For trajectories: medoid series plots, cluster sizes by domain, transition matrices, and case-study field-flow plots drawn from medoids, not hand-picked.",
"practice_alignment": "MEETS: (a) Entry is modelled on risk sets of not-yet-entered fields with lagged regressors, using a conditional logit stratified by concept-year, with relatedness density (Hidalgo), phi_home, log field size and the target field's own gateway centrality as baselines, all on the same frozen 1998-2002 backbone. The candidate (d) enters only as an added block, tested by LR. (b) The backbone predates every concept onset (1998-2002 < 2003). (c) Concept-clustered bootstrap CIs with 2,000 draws; per-group random-effects pooling with I^2 and a sign count. (d) Label-permutation null (1,000 draws) and degree-preserving rewired-backbone null for the gateway score (200 draws). (e) The rescue test follows the ecological design: retention vs connectivity to occupied neighbour fields, field size held fixed, within-concept FE, plus a mediation check. (f) Citation provenance is background-adjusted with the same citing papers' non-concept references, which answers M1. Shared-author links are removed, following art_xp8BGBJZsxeI. (g) Trajectory k is chosen by silhouette plus bootstrap ARI >= 0.6. The DTW-medoid taxonomy is frozen on dev and replicated on held-out data (assignment vs independent reclustering, ARI). (h) The ordering test uses a detector calibrated to 5% false alarms on shuffled series, a peripheral-field matched contrast and an event-study lead-lag panel with concept FE. (i) Hold-out is sealed: frozen_spec.json and its SHA-256 are written before any held-out outcome or entry column is computed.\n\nDEPARTURES. (1) 26 fields instead of the hundreds of categories common in relatedness studies (e.g. 252 subfields or PACS codes). Justified: gateway_j is frozen at the 26-field level from iteration 1, and venue-source profiles give reliable labels only at the field level. Cost: risk sets are small (<= 25 alternatives), entry of big fields is nearly certain, and density and size are collinear. Mitigation: target-field fixed effects in an LPM robustness model, so that only within-field variation across concepts identifies (d). (2) Entry and retention use yearly grounded-count thresholds (>= 2 works) rather than revealed comparative advantage (RCA > 1), the norm in economic complexity. Justified: concept volumes per field are tiny, so RCA is unstable. Cost: entry is partly a size event. Mitigation: an RCA-based entry definition as a robustness row (entry when the field's share of the concept exceeds its share of all works, with >= 2 works). (3) Title-only matching (no abstracts) for the full-corpus pass. Justified: the 6 h budget; abstracts are the largest column. Cost: lower recall, especially in fields with short titles. Mitigation: label coverage and matched-count covariates; the recall of title-only vs tag-only is reported from the benchmark. (4) Legacy-concept lexicon, a survivorship-selected vocabulary named by about 2019. Episode-level tests use concept FE, so selection acts on concepts, not on fields within concepts; concept-level results carry the caveat. (5) The frame is built independently of the authoritative S1 dataset artifact, because experiments may only depend on datasets and none exists yet. Cost: possible disagreement in home or t0. frame_concepts.csv and episodes.csv use the S1 columns so that iteration 3 can join them and report agreement. (6) The mediation analysis for rescue is observational (a product-of-coefficients with bootstrap), not causal; reported as consistent-with, never as proof. (7) The direction's budget line says '$0 OpenRouter', but its grounding step asks for LLM labels (<= $1). I spend <= $0.50 on <= 400 labels (expected about $0.05) and log this in deviations.json. (8) The lexicon prefilter P0 removes concepts already common in titles before 2003, from a 5% file sample. This is outcome-blind (it uses only years before any eligible t0) and follows from the rule 't0 = first year with >= 20 grounded works, t0 in 2003-2014'. It may drop polysemous re-emerging senses, and the count dropped is reported.",

DEV_HOME = {17: CS, 22: Eng, 13: BGM, 27: Med}. HELDOUT_GROUP = {Physical: [15, 16, 21, 25, 31]; LifeEnv: [11, 19, 23, 24, 28, 30]; Social: [12, 14, 20, 32, 33]; MathDec: [18, 26]; OtherHealth: [29, 34, 35, 36] (held-out, pooled, reported separately, not one of the 4 sign-test groups)}. BASE = type in {article, review}, is_paratext false, is_xpac false if the column exists, years 1995-2022. M_RAREFY = 30 (50 as a sensitivity). EPISODE_MIN = 2. RET_MIN = 2.\n\nSTEP 0: SETUP AND SCHEMA PROBE (<= 15 min)\n0.1 Copy the reused files listed in builds_on into inputs/ and lib/, and log a SHA-256 per file in results/inputs_manifest.json.\n0.2 Read the parquet footer of 1 works file with rangefile.py and dump the leaf column paths to results/works_schema.json. Resolve the exact leaf names for: id; title (or display_name); publication_year; type; is_paratext; is_xpac; primary_location.source.id; primary_topic.field.id; topics.list.element.id; concepts.list.element.id; concepts.list.element.score; authorships.list.element.author.id; referenced_works.list.element.\n0.3 If the concepts columns are ABSENT, set TAGS=False: grounding then relies on the sense filter only (Step 3), and log the deviation.\n0.4 Lexicon: read the concepts entity from s3://openalex/data/parquet/concepts (manifest.json; 0 credits). If it is missing, cursor-page /concepts with select=id,display_name,level,wikidata,description (about 330 calls, 1 credit each; count them). Keep levels 2-5 with a wikidata id. Drop display names that are a single token of <= 3 characters, and drop the level-0/1 discipline names. Aliases: Wikidata English aliases via wbgetentities (50 ids per call, free, 4 req/s). Keep aliases of >= 2 tokens, or single tokens of >= 6 characters that are not English dictionary words (use the wordfreq zipf < 3.5 test). Write lexicon.parquet (concept_idx, openalex_id, name, aliases, level, wikidata, description) and FREEZE it (SHA-256 into results/lexicon_hash.txt) before any count is taken.\n\nSTEP 1: PREFILTER P0 (outcome-blind, <= 15 min)\nScan a seeded 5% random subset of the works files (about 100 files) for title + year + base columns. Match surface forms with Aho-Corasick (below). For each concept, estimate full-corpus yearly title hits for 1998-2002 as sample hits / 0.05. DROP a concept if the estimated count is >= 200 in any year 1998-2002 (10x the t0 threshold, so t0 would be < 2003 even at 10% grounding precision). Also drop surface forms that hit > 0.5% of all sampled titles (generic words). Write p0_dropped.csv with reasons. Expected: 65k -> about 15-25k concepts; if > 30k remain, tighten to >= 100.\n\nMATCHER (shared by Steps 1, 2, 4)\n- Surface forms per concept: name + aliases, lowercased and passed through scan_snapshot.normalise (hyphens and slashes to spaces, possessives stripped), plus simple plural/singular variants (s/es/ies) of the last token.\n- Build one pyahocorasick automaton over ' form ' strings padded with spaces, to force word boundaries. Titles are normalised the same way and padded.\n- Every raw hit is verified with the stem-positional phrase_spec/match_title logic of scan_snapshot.py (lemma-aware, stop-word gaps allowed).\n- Keep per hit: concept_idx and match_type in {exact, variant}.\n\nSTEP 2: PASS 1, FULL-CORPUS COUNT PASS (target <= 45 min, 4 workers)\nColumns: publication_year, type, is_paratext, is_xpac, title, primary_location.source.id, primary_topic.field.id, concepts id+score.\nPer file:\n(a) base mask; yearly base totals G[y]; venue-field yearly totals GF[y, f] via the source_field map (a numpy LUT over source int ids; build it as a dict -> sorted arrays + searchsorted).\n(b) Aho-Corasick verified hits on base works only.\n(c) For each hit (work w, concept c), compute these flags:\n    - tag = c in w's concept ids with score >= 0.3 (use the legacy concept int id);\n    - notags = w has no concept tags at all;\n    - vf = venue field of w (or -1);\n    - pf = primary_topic field.\n(d) Accumulate sparse counters over keys (c, year, vf) for: n_title, n_tagtitle (tag), n_notags (notags). Also accumulate n_tagonly(c, year) = tagged with score >= 0.3 but no title hit, computed vectorially from the flattened concepts column restricted to the P0 lexicon ids. Use np.unique on int64 keys c*10^4 + (year-1995)*100 + (vf+1), then add into a global dict of numpy arrays. Keep a pf version only for n_tagtitle.\n(e) Reservoir-sample up to 40 matched titles per concept (title, year, vf, tag flag, match_type, work row position) for the benchmark.\nCheckpoint every 100 files (resume logic from scan_snapshot.py). Staged: run --limit 20, time it and extrapolate to 2,040 files. If the extrapolation is > 60 min, drop the pf counter and the reservoir to 20.\n\nSTEP 3: GROUNDING (existing resources first, then a small labelled benchmark)\n3.1 Existing resource = the legacy concept tag. The primary grounded count is g(c, y, f) = n_tagtitle + n_notags x p_sense (the sense filter applied only to works without any tags). Rules compared on the benchmark: title-only, exact-only, lemma-variant, tag-only (tag without title), tag AND title.\n3.2 Benchmark: 400 (concept, title) pairs from the reservoirs, stratified by match type (exact/variant), tag flag (yes/no/notags) and field domain. Draw from DEV-home and held-out-home concepts alike (labels concern meaning, not outcomes).\n    - Label with one cheap OpenRouter model (pick the cheapest instruction-following model via aii-openrouter-llms, e.g. a flash-lite class model; confirm its price first). Prompt: concept name + Wikidata description + title -> {yes, no, unsure} JSON.\n    - A second, different cheap model double-labels 150 pairs; report Cohen's kappa.\n    - The executor hand-checks 60 pairs by reading them itself and records its own labels in benchmark/hand_labels.csv.\n    - Split 240/160 train/test (the direction's 300/200 is scaled to <= 400 labels; logged).\n    - Budget estimate before starting: 550 calls x about 400 tokens = 0.22M tokens, about $0.03-0.10.\n    - If a sibling dataset artifact already holds a grounding benchmark, reuse it and label only its missing strata.\n3.3 Sense filter: logistic regression on [cosine(MiniLM(title), MiniLM(name + description)), exact flag, variant flag, tag flag, tag score, n_tokens of the form, concept level]. Report test precision, recall and AUC for each rule and for the filter. Freeze sense_filter.pkl.\n3.4 Per-concept precision estimate = mean filter probability over that concept's reservoir titles (<= 40), restricted to titles that the chosen grounding rule accepts. DROP concepts with estimated precision < 0.8 BEFORE any outcome is computed. Write grounding_report.json.\n\nSTEP 4: FRAME, EPISODES AND CONCEPT OUTCOMES (tables, no model fitting yet)\n4.1 From yearly grounded totals g(c, y) = sum_f g(c, y, f) (all venue fields including unlabelled), compute t0 with s0_ground.onset (first year 2000..2014 with >= 20; newborn relative rule; non-newborn = re-emerging stratum, excluded from the main analyses). Keep t0 in 2003-2014 and sum g over t0..t0+2 >= 30.\n4.2 Home = s0_labels.home_of applied to the venue fields of the FIRST 30 grounded venue-labelled works. This needs per-work order, which the counts do not give, so approximate it by cumulative yearly counts up to the year in which 30 labelled works are reached, taking the final year proportionally. Flag home_weak if no field reaches 40%. intersection_born = 1 if there are >= 2 home fields. primary group = the largest home field -> DEV / heldout group.\n4.3 split = 'dev' if t0 in 2003-2009 and group in DEV_HOME; 'heldout_field' if t0 in 2003-2009 and the group is held-out; 'heldout_cohort' if t0 in 2010-2014 (any home).\n4.4 frame_concepts.csv. Columns: concept_id, name, level, t0, newborn, home (list), home_weak, intersection_born, group, split, n_early, label_coverage_early, precision_est, in_P78 flag, plus the outcome columns O1, O3, O2r_m30, O2r_m50, O2_raw, N_outcome, O2r_resid.\n    SEALING: outcome columns are written ONLY for dev rows now; held-out rows get NaN until Step 8.\n    O2r_resid = residual of O2r on log n_early, fitted on dev only; the coefficients are stored in the frozen spec.\n4.5 episodes.csv, one row per (c, j) with j not in home and g(c, j, t0..t0+2) >= 2. Columns: n_early_j, entry_year e_j (first year cumulative >= 2), gateway_j (plus deg and btw variants), phi_home_j, log_size_j (GF over t0-3..t0-1), background retention propensity (filled in Step 6), label_coverage, R_cj (>= 2 grounded works in j in t0+6..t0+8; dev only until sealing).\n4.6 Targets: >= 400 concepts, >= 4,000 episodes. If fewer: relax the P0 threshold to >= 400; then allow t0 in 2003-2014 with n_early >= 20 (logged). Report the counts per split and group.\n4.7 Agreement with iteration 1: for P78 concepts present, report Spearman of O2r and t0 against gen_art_experiment_4/outcomes.csv, and home agreement.\n4.8 API audit (<= 60 credits): for 40 random frame concepts, one group_by=publication_year call with title.search on the quoted name + BASE filter. Report the Spearman of yearly counts and the ratio of snapshot grounded to API counts in t0..t0+2.\n\nSTEP 5: PASS 2, ROWS FOR FRAME CONCEPTS (target <= 70 min)\n5.1 Columns: id, publication_year, type, is_paratext, title, primary_location.source.id, primary_topic.field.id, topics ids (first 3), concepts id+score, authorships author ids, referenced_works.\n5.2 Match titles against the FRAME lexicon only, and apply the SAME grounding rule.\n5.3 Store matched rows as parquet parts: work_id int64, year, vf, pf, concept list, author ids, referenced ids int64, topic ids.\n5.4 In the same pass, append (work_id, vf) for ALL base works with vf >= 0 and year 1990-2022 into per-file npy parts. These become the global id->field map for background references: sort once and use searchsorted, about 2.5-3 GB RAM.\n5.5 Staged: time --limit 20 first. If the projected time is > 90 min, read referenced_works/authorships from a seeded 50% of files and log it as missing-at-random coverage. Rescue and relay then use shares and availability-normalised counts, so that thinning does not bias them.\n\nSTEP 6: DEV ANALYSES (only split == dev is loaded; the loader asserts it)\n6.0 Field-year state machine per concept (years t0-2..t0+8), for each field k:\n    - entered(k, t) = cumulative g >= 2 by t;\n    - retaining(k, t) = entered by t-2 AND g(k, t-2..t) >= 2 AND k not in home;\n    - lost(k, t) = entered AND g(k, t-2..t) == 0.\n    Build generic retention propensity P_j^(-c) = the share of OTHER same-cohort (t0 +/- 2) concepts' episodes in j that were retained (leave-this-concept-out; dev only).\n6.1 H2 NEXT-FIELD ENTRY\n    - Risk sets: for t = t0+1..t0+8 (bounded by 2022), candidates k not entered by t-1 and not home. Event: entered at t. Stratum = (c, t).\n    - PRIMARY sample = strata with Ret(t-1) non-empty ('after first off-home retention'). SECONDARY = all strata, with an indicator for empty Ret.\n    - Regressors, standardised on dev:\n      (a) phi_home(k) = mean_h phi[h, k];\n      (b) log size_k(t-1) from GF;\n      (c) Hidalgo density to all entered fields E(t-1): sum_{j in E} phi[j, k] / sum_j phi[j, k];\n      (e) g_k (own gateway);\n      (d0) mean_{j in Ret} phi[j, k];\n      (d) sum_{j in Ret(t-1)} g_j phi[j, k] / sum_{j in Ret} g_j.\n    - Also a PLACEBO block (d_lost): the same form over LOST fields.\n    - Estimator: own vectorised conditional logit (each event contributes x_e.beta - log sum_{risk set} exp(x.beta); np.add.reduceat; scipy L-BFGS with analytic gradient). Validate against statsmodels ConditionalLogit on a 300-stratum subset (coefficients within 2%).\n    - Models: M0 = a+b+c+e; M1 = M0 + d0; M2 = M0 + d; M3 = M0 + d0 + d.\n    - LR tests: M2 vs M0 (primary), M3 vs M1 (does gateway WEIGHTING add beyond retaining relatedness?), and the phi_home coefficient in M2 (the relatedness-to-home comparison).\n    - Per-stratum AUC of each block's linear predictor; mean within-stratum AUC with concept-clustered bootstrap CIs (2,000 draws; resample concepts, relabel strata).\n    - Placebos:\n      (i) permute field labels of (phi, g) jointly, 1,000 draws; refit M2; LR distribution.\n      (ii) degree-preserving rewired backbone: threshold phi > 0, double-edge swaps (networkx.double_edge_swap, nswap = 10 x edges) with the original weights reassigned to the rewired edges, recompute eigenvector centrality g', recompute (d); 200 draws. Expect no gain.\n      (iii) d_lost should not beat d.\n    - Robustness rows:\n      - LPM with pyfixest feols('entered ~ a+b+c+e+d | concept^t + field', vcov={'CRV1': 'concept'});\n      - RCA-based entry definition;\n      - pf labels instead of vf;\n      - excluding intersection-born concepts;\n      - boundary stratum: home is a top-tercile gateway vs not (interaction d x gateway_home).\n6.2 RESCUE (episodes with e_j <= t0+2)\n    - Provenance window W = t0+3..t0+5, BEFORE the outcome window t0+6..t0+8.\n    - For each grounded paper p in field j in W: take its references to grounded c-papers (from the matched rows) published in [year(p)-5, year(p)]. Drop pairs that share any author id (these form the self-lineage channel, reported separately). Classify each cited paper's vf as HOME (in H), SELF (== j), OTHER (not home, != j) or unlabelled (dropped).\n    - Raw shares per episode: s_other = OTHER / (HOME + OTHER); s_self = SELF / total.\n    - Background: the same citing papers' non-concept references (all refs minus c-refs; sample <= 10 per paper; fields via the global id->field map). The same classes are used, with 'home' = the concept's home fields.\n    - Background-adjusted log-odds contrast: resc_cj = log((OTHER_c + 0.5) / (HOME_c + 0.5)) - log((OTHER_bg + 0.5) / (HOME_bg + 0.5)).\n    - Hanski connectivity (ecological rescue): S_cj = sum_{k occupied at t0..t0+2, k not in H, k != j} phi[j, k] * log(1 + g(c, k, t0..t0+2)).\n    - Tests, with concept FE and CRV1 by concept (pyfixest):\n      R1: resc_cj ~ retained x gate_tercile + log n_early_j + log size_j | concept. Rescue predicts a positive retained x top-tercile interaction. Also the raw s_other version.\n      R2: retained ~ gate_j + log size_j + phi_home_j + P_j^(-c) + label_cov + log n_early_j | concept (LPM; the H1 replication inside this frame), then adding S_cj and resc_cj. Mediation = the drop in the gate_j coefficient, with a concept-bootstrap CI for the indirect effect (2,000 draws).\n      R3: P(retained) vs S_cj, binned plot (the incidence-function curve), for gateway and peripheral terciles.\n6.3 RELAY\n    - For each episode (c, j) and each later-entered field k (k not in H, k != j, e_k in [e_j+1, min(e_j+5, t0+8)]): hit_jk = 1 if any of k's first 5 grounded papers cites (non-shared-author) a c-paper in j published before e_k.\n    - relay_cj = sum_k hit_jk.\n    - Availability null: E_cj = sum_k [1 - (1 - a_jk)^{m_k}], where a_jk = j's share of all grounded c-papers published before e_k, and m_k = the number of c-refs in k's first 5 papers.\n    - relay_excess = relay_cj - E_cj.\n    - Test: pyfixest fepois('relay ~ retained*gate_tercile + log n_early_j + log size_j | concept', vcov CRV1 concept), offset log(E_cj + 0.1) as a sensitivity. Also OLS on relay_excess.\n    - Optional zero-cost covariate if author ids are present: n_unconnected_components among j's early adopters (Cheng et al. style; coauthorship graph among the early authors in j) — candidate S as a rival.\n6.4 TRAJECTORIES (dev concepts with O1 = 1; the intersection-born stratum is separate)\n    - Yearly series t0..t0+8 (9 points), each z-scored within variable across concepts:\n      - n_entered_offhome(t);\n      - n_retaining(t);\n      - n_lost(t);\n      - rarefied richness R20(t) of the 3-year rolling venue-field distribution (m = 20, NaN if N < 20, forward-filled);\n      - Shannon entropy H(t) of the same window;\n      - gateway share G_t = the gateway-weighted share of off-home works;\n      - log volume(t);\n      - if pass 2 topics exist: ego participation and ego betweenness per year, from gen_art_experiment_3 features.py functions on the per-year concept-topic PMI ego network (backbone slice by year).\n    - DTW: tslearn.metrics.cdist_dtw, Sakoe-Chiba radius 2, on the multivariate series. KMedoids (PAM) with k = 2..8. Silhouette on the DTW matrix. Bootstrap stability: 100 resamples of 80% of concepts, ARI on the overlapping concepts. Choose the k with the maximum silhouette among k with median ARI >= 0.6; if none qualify, report k = 2 and flag 'unstable'.\n    - HMM: hmmlearn GaussianHMM (diag covariance) on the same series pooled across concepts, n_states 2..6 by BIC; decode per concept; cross-tab the most frequent state path against the DTW clusters (ARI).\n    - Name the clusters AFTER fitting by their medoid shapes against the brief's candidates (localized, rapid interdisciplinary, gradual integration, transient, brokerage).\n    - Store the medoid series and freeze them.\n6.5 ORDERING\n    - Entropy take-off tau_c: the first upward change in H(t) found by ruptures Pelt (rbf or l2, min_size 2) with penalty pen chosen so that the false-alarm rate on SHUFFLED series (years permuted within concept, 200 shuffles over the bottom-O2r-tercile dev concepts) is 5%; require the post-segment mean to exceed the pre-segment mean.\n    - gamma_c = the first year some top-tercile-gateway off-home field is retaining; pi_c = the same for a bottom-tercile (peripheral) field.\n    - Among dev top-tercile O2r concepts (tercile cutoffs frozen from dev), p_gw = share with gamma_c < tau_c (ties reported separately and excluded), with a sign test. Matched contrast: the share with pi_c < tau_c; McNemar-style paired comparison.\n    - Lead-lag event study: pyfixest feols('dH_{t+1} ~ ret_gw_t + ret_per_t + log vol_t | concept + age', CRV1 concept), plus the reverse regression (d ret_gw_{t+1} ~ H_t), plus leads and lags -3..+3 of the first-retained-gateway event (Sun-Abraham style: pyfixest event_study, or manual dummies with never-treated concepts as controls).\n    - Placebo: permute g across fields, 200 draws; the lead-lag coefficient must vanish.\n6.6 POWER CHECK on dev: from the concept-bootstrap distribution of the M2 vs M0 LR statistic, estimate P(p < 0.01) at the held-out n (the number of held-out concepts). If below 0.8, say so in frozen_spec.json. The rule does not change.\n\nSTEP 7: FREEZE\nWrite frozen_spec.json with:\n- the regressor definitions; the standardisation means and SDs (dev); the model formulas M0-M3 and R1-R3;\n- the tercile cutoffs (gateway; O2r, via dev O2r_resid coefficients);\n- the change-point penalty; the DTW medoids + k + HMM params;\n- the sense-filter hash, the lexicon hash, the backbone hash and the code hash (sha256 of all .py files);\n- the decision rules:\n  - H2-entry CONFIRMED on held-out if the LR M2 vs M0 has p < 0.01 AND the pooled d coefficient is > 0 with concept-bootstrap 95% CI > 0 AND the sign is positive in >= 3 of 4 held-out groups plus the cohort AND the permutation p < 0.05 AND the rewired placebo gain is <= the 95th percentile of its null;\n  - H2-ordering CONFIRMED if p_gw >= 0.60 with a one-sided sign test p < 0.05 AND p_gw > the peripheral share;\n  - RESCUE SUPPORTED if the R1 interaction is > 0 with a CI > 0 AND the R2 indirect effect is > 0;\n  - RELAY SUPPORTED if the retained x top-gateway fepois coefficient is > 0 with a CI > 0 AND the mean relay_excess of gateway-retained episodes is > 0.\nLog sha256(frozen_spec.json) to results/freeze_log.txt with a timestamp. Only then compute the held-out outcome and entry columns.\n\nSTEP 8: HELD-OUT, ONCE\nLoad the heldout_field and heldout_cohort splits, and compute their outcomes and state machines. Run 6.1-6.5 EXACTLY with the frozen spec (no refitting of the standardisation; coefficients are re-estimated only as the confirmatory estimate — the frozen model is also scored for its prediction AUC with dev coefficients). Per group (Physical, LifeEnv, Social, MathDec, Cohort; OtherHealth reported): coefficient of d with a concept-bootstrap CI, then DerSimonian-Laird pooling, I^2 and the sign count. Trajectories: assign held-out O1 = 1 concepts to the frozen medoids; also recluster held-out independently with the same k, and report the ARI with the frozen assignment and the cluster proportions by group. Write heldout_result.json. Never rerun this step after inspecting it; if a bug is found, fix it, rerun once, and log both runs.\n\nSTEP 9: CASE STUDIES, FIGURES AND OUTPUTS\n9.1 Pick 4-6 case concepts = the dev and held-out cluster medoids plus the extreme relay_excess episodes.\n9.2 Field-flow figure per case: x = year, y = the 26 fields sorted by gateway_eig; marker size = grounded works; colour = state (home/entered/retaining/lost); arrows = relay citations j -> k. Use aii-data-fig-gen style or matplotlib, as PDF + PNG.\n9.3 Other figures: the entry-block AUC forest by group; the incidence-function curve (R3); medoid series panels; the event-study plot; placebo histograms.\n9.4 method_out.json in the exp_gen_sol_out schema (validate with aii-json; make full/mini/preview variants). datasets:\n    - 'entry_events_dev' and 'entry_events_heldout': examples = candidate-field rows with input (concept, year, field, regressors), output (entered), predict_M0 and predict_M2 (linear predictors or probabilities);\n    - 'retention_episodes': predict_base and predict_with_connectivity.\n    Also include a metadata block with every headline number.\n9.5 Also write results/: frame_concepts.csv, episodes.csv, entry_risk_sets.parquet, rescue.csv, relay.csv, trajectories.csv, cluster_assign.csv, dev_result.json, heldout_result.json, grounding_report.json, agreement.json, deviations.json, credits_log.csv, openrouter_cost.json.\n9.6 README.md (layout, how to run, 'Restoring removed files') and .aii/manifest.yaml: keep results/ and the pass1/pass2 aggregates (not reproducible because the snapshot drifts); delete .venv, the HF model cache and __pycache__ as regenerable or redownloadable; the global id->field npy is delete: regenerable (source: uv run pass2.py --idmap-only). Run aii-file-size-limit on any JSON > the limit.",
"fallback_plan": "F1 (snapshot throughput too slow; extrapolated pass 1 > 60 min or pass 2 > 90 min). Pass 1: drop the pf counter and the reservoir to 20 per concept. Pass 2: read referenced_works/authorships from a seeded 50% of files (then 25%), logged as missing-at-random coverage; rescue and relay use shares and the availability-normalised relay, which thinning does not bias. If still too slow, restrict pass 2 to files whose pass-1 match count for frame concepts is in the top 60% (log the coverage). H2 entry and the trajectories need ONLY pass-1 counts, so they are never blocked by pass 2.\n\nF2 (no legacy concepts column). TAGS=False: grounding = title match + sense filter for all works. The benchmark grows to 400 pairs over the frame concepts only, and the per-concept precision gate stays at 0.8. The lexicon comes from the concepts entity or the /concepts API (<= 330 credits).\n\nF3 (frame too small: < 400 concepts or < 4,000 episodes). Relax P0 to >= 400, then n_early to >= 20, then EPISODE_MIN stays 2 but the entry threshold becomes cumulative >= 2 over t0..t0+3 (logged). If still < 250 concepts, run H2 pooled dev + held-out with a group random effect as EXPLORATORY and state that confirmation was impossible.\n\nF4 (conditional logit does not converge or separates). Ridge-penalised clogit (L2 = 1e-3 on standardised coefficients); or the pyfixest LPM with concept^year FE as the primary estimator, keeping the same decision rule on the d coefficient.\n\nF5 (the rewired placebo is degenerate because the 26-node positive-PMI graph is near-complete and swaps fail). Use a weight-reshuffling null that preserves node strength (a Maslov-Sneppen analogue on the weighted matrix via the 'strength-preserving' algorithm of Rubinov & Sporns 2011, or a simple iterative proportional fitting of permuted weights to the original strengths), and log it.\n\nF6 (trajectory clustering unstable, ARI < 0.6 for all k). Report 'no stable taxonomy'. Fall back to HMM states as the descriptive taxonomy, and to a 2-way split on the first principal component of the DTW embedding (classical MDS). Do not name classes.\n\nF7 (change-point detector calibration fails: too few non-diffusing concepts). Calibrate on shuffled series of ALL dev concepts. If entropy take-off is undetected for > 50% of broad concepts, use the threshold-free lead-lag panel as the primary ordering test and report the detector result as secondary.\n\nF8 (OpenRouter unavailable or budget refused). Use the 60 hand-checked pairs plus tag-agreement as a silver label (tag AND title = positive; title without tag on a tagged work with a different high-score sense = negative) to train the sense filter. Report it as silver-labelled.\n\nF9 (run volume not mounted). Rebuild the backbone (27 credits, backbone.py logic) and source_field (sources snapshot); copy rangefile/scan logic from the definitions in this plan; log every rebuilt input.\n\nF10 (time overrun near 5 h). Priority order to keep: Step 4 tables > 6.1 H2 + freeze + held-out H2 > 6.4/6.5 trajectories and ordering > 6.2 rescue > 6.3 relay > case figures. Anything skipped is reported as NOT RUN, never as null.",
"testing_plan": "T0 UNIT TESTS (tests/test_units.py, < 2 min):\n- the Aho-Corasick + stem verification matches the iteration-1 match_title on 500 P78-matched titles from the gen_art_experiment_3 match spec (agreement >= 99%);\n- plural variants hit, and 'in vitro' does not match 'invitrogen' (word boundaries);\n- rarefied_richness vs Monte Carlo (within 0.02);\n- onset() on synthetic series (newborn, re-emerging, never >= 20);\n- the state machine on a hand-made field-year table;\n- the own conditional logit vs statsmodels ConditionalLogit on 300 synthetic strata (coefficients within 2%);\n- a planted positive control: simulate entries where P(enter k) is proportional to exp(1.0 x d) with the real phi/g and real risk-set structure; the LR must detect it at p < 0.001 with n = 300 concepts; with the beta on d set to 0, the rejection rate at 0.01 over 100 simulations must be <= 3%;\n- rescue: a planted episode set where retained gateway episodes cite OTHER fields 2x more; R1 must recover it;\n- relay: availability-null expectation vs simulation (within 5%);\n- DTW clustering recovers 3 planted trajectory shapes (ARI > 0.8);\n- change-point calibration yields a 5% +/- 2% false-alarm rate on fresh shuffles.\n\nT1 MINI RUN (20 works files, about 1% of the corpus; <= 10 min):\n- check the parquet column names resolve;\n- pass-1 counters are non-empty;\n- time per file -> extrapolation logged;\n- the 10 known P78 concepts (e.g. 'optogenetics', 'smart grid', 'crowdsourcing') appear with plausible year profiles;\n- the tag-AND-title / title ratio is in the 0.3-0.95 range reported by the probe.\nSTOP and debug if any known concept has zero hits.\n\nT2 SCALE-UP: 100 files, then 500 files, then all (the aii-long-running-tasks pattern), running in the background with a PID and checkpoints; No domain handbook covers scientometrics, so this rests on those sources and on standard knowledge of the literatures named below.\\n\\n(1) BASELINES A REVIEWER EXPECTS. Next-entry (diversification) studies in economic complexity and science-of-science (Hidalgo et al. 2007; Neffke, Henning & Boschma 2011; Guevara et al. 2016; Chinazzi et al. 2019; the 2020 Sci Rep portfolio study) always model entry of a not-yet-active unit into a category at t as a function of RELATEDNESS DENSITY to the categories it already holds at t-1. They control for category size or ubiquity and sometimes the unit's own size. The model is a linear probability model with unit-by-time fixed effects or a conditional logit, with SEs clustered by unit. The first baseline a reviewer names is relatedness density itself (Hidalgo). The next are the target category's size and ubiquity, since big fields get entered more; iteration 1 found log size (AUC 0.74) beats density (0.61). To be fair, the rival must use the same backbone (the same phi) and the same risk sets as the candidate, and all regressors must be standardised. For a claim that POSITION rather than relatedness matters, reviewers also want the target field's own centrality as a control, a label-permutation null, and a configuration or rewired null for the centrality score.\\n\\n(2) DATA. OpenAlex is now standard: open, 476M works, venue sources with topic profiles, referenced_works and author ids. Its known issues, recorded in this run: field-dependent reference coverage; low venue-label coverage for conference-heavy CS (26-80%); legacy concept tags are MAG-classifier output (deprecated but still present); document-type noise. The OpenAlex topics/primary_topic classifier reads the paper's own text and references, so it is used only as a sensitivity label. Curated emergence lists (Research Fronts) are proprietary. MeSH is biomedical only.\\n\\n(3) CONTROLS. Entry models hold the backbone fixed in a pre-period to avoid simultaneity (here 1998-2002, frozen in iteration 1). Retention or extinction studies from metapopulation ecology (Hanski 1994 incidence function model; Brown & Kodric-Brown 1977 rescue effect) test rescue by showing that local extinction probability FALLS with connectivity to occupied patches, S_i = sum_j w_ij * occupied_j. They hold patch size (area) fixed, because big patches both persist and are well connected; the scientometric analogue is field size. The confound this design is most likely to be caught on: gateway fields are big, generically retentive and adopt everything (iteration-1 M1: 66-72% of raw lineage is homophily). So every mechanism statistic must be (a) within-concept (concept FE), (b) size-controlled, and (c) background-adjusted for citation provenance (same citing papers' non-concept references).\\n\\n(4) HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH. Entry studies use thousands to hundreds of thousands of unit-category-period rows and report clustered CIs. With 26 fields a concept-year risk set has at most 25 alternatives, so power comes from concepts: >= 300 concepts and >= 2,000 entry events are needed for stable conditional-logit coefficients with concept-clustered bootstrap CIs. Iteration 1 had 28-46 concepts and is not believed. Trajectory clustering is believed only with a stability check (bootstrap ARI >= 0.6) and a reported silhouette; k chosen visually is not accepted. Ordering claims need a detector calibrated to a false-alarm rate plus a threshold-free lead-lag panel, because threshold detectors produce spurious orderings when the series have different noise.\\n\\n(5) REPORTING. Conditional-logit or LPM coefficient tables with standardised coefficients and concept-clustered 95% CIs. LR tests of nested blocks. Mean within-stratum AUC of each block.
